AMALIENBORG: US-built chemical tanker for Denmark

Shipbuilder: ...Alabama Shipyard Inc, USA

VeESSEIl'S NAMEL...... cece erences Amalienborg
Hull NUMDES: oo... cece es ou iis surat ste eesie cans 46

Designer:...... Skipskonsulent A/S, Norway

CCORDING to the Maritime Administration, malienborg is the first ocean-going vessel to be built in the USA for export for 40 years (the other contender for that honour, the first Eletson tanker from Newport
News - American Progress, Significant Ships of 1997 - was sold to US operator Mobil prior to delivery). The design, developed by Norwegian consultant Skipskonsulent, conforms to the single-deck/double-hull layout, with troughed centreline and transverse bulkheads dividing the cargo space into 12 tanks, two slop tanks and a small catgo drain tank. With deck stiffening arranged exter- nally, and tank-side structures contained within the dou- ble skin, the tanks are plain sided for easy cleaning and maintenance, and have been coated with Sigma Phen- ouatd epoxy.

Cargo is handled through stainless steel piping using
Framo submerged pumps in each tank. The side tanks incorporate sloped top and bottom wings, and together with the double-bottom tanks (except two utilised for technical water) ate designated for water ballast. Fuel is cattied in bunkers abreast the machinery space, separated from the shell by cofferdams.

The machinery arrangement is centred upon a Wartt-

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 sila 6L46A main engine developing 4800kW at 500rev/min MCR for a speed of 14.5knots. At 85% full powet, service speed is 13.7 knots. The drive to a
Kamewa CP propeller is through a Valmet gearbox which reduces engine speed to 100rev/min and also has a take-off shaft for a Leroy Somer 1125kVA alternator running at 1800rev/min. Two Wartsila/Leroy Somer 1105kVA diesel-driven alternators are also fitted.
Kamewa supplied the 588kW/384trev/min bow thruster and the rudder is of the Ulstein enhanced-action flapped type.

Accommodation is provided for a total complement of 18 in a four-tier deckhouse built on the poop. Above this the fully enclosed wheelhouse projects forward at the centreline and features a two-seat conning console.
Each cabin has a private toilet, and public rooms avail- able include a mess and day room, officers' dayroom and a captain's/officers' conference room. A cargo control room and an engine control room are also provided.
Behind the deckhouse the separate funnel casing is off- set to port, allowing a stern-launching Norsafe fully enclosed lifeboat and ramp to be positioned to star-
 board.

ARATERE: versatile ferry for New Zealand service
 shipbuilder: ......... Hijos de J Barreras SA, -5 en Motes neg «nda eyes’ arya wn 92D 8 wars Spain
VESSEI'S NAME? .... eee ceccseeeeeeeeeeees Aratere
Hull number: wo. ee ceyue newsre seman 1570
Owner/operator: ......... ee. Tranz Rail Ltd, eee eee eee eeeeeeeeesueceeeeueeueeueueeeueseennn New Zealand
Designer: ...Hijos de J Barreras SA, Spain
FLAG! ccc eeceeccseeceeeeseeesseseeceueeeuveveeenees Bahamas
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: wo... eee, Nil
Total number of sister

Ships still ON OFder: wo... eee eeeeeeeee ees Nil

Nog. privately owned, the Barreras shipyard never- theless still retains strong links with its former par- ent, AESA, an established leader in the ferry industry, as a tesult of a series of designs successfully imple- menting the many regulations instituted in the wake of the Herald of Free Enterprise, and other recent disasters.
Alratere, therefore, is a one-compartment-standard ves- sel with a pedigree, complying with the requirements of
Stab90, including withstanding 500mm of water on the main deck, and meeting the demands of the Stockholm rules.

The Intet-islander service across the Cook Strait between Wellington and Picton on which Aratere will setve, covets 51 miles and includes 26 miles of open sea subject to notoriously bad weather conditions. To counter these, a pair of Brown Brothers fin stabilisers, and an Intering air-activated heel control system which uses anti-heeling tanks of 860m? capacity, and includes test equipment enabling stability conditions to be con- firmed before sailing, are fitted.

The vessel can accommodate 355 day passengers, plus 20 drivers carried in two-berth cabins with private toilet facilities, and having their own mess and TV lounge. For the remainder, there are a series of lounges, a self-service cafeteria, tavern bar, cinema, TV and writ- ing rooms, and outdoor seating, 
Simultaneous access over shore-based linkspans is arranged directly over the stern for rail wagons and toad vehicles on to the train (main) deck, and for vehi- cles only, to the partially enclosed vehicle deck above. A tamp and door in the aft port quarter provides an alter- native entry for vehicles to the train deck. A fixed ramp links that deck with a lower hold having a capacity for 70 cars, and a hydraulically operated ramp connects the train and vehicle decks.

Four recessed rail tracks provide 425 lane metres for parking rail or road vehicles on the train deck, and 580 lane metres are available for trucks on the vehicle deck. All spaces (except the hold) can carry dangerous cargoes other than Class 6.2 (infectious substances), with the train deck suitable for A,B and E stowage cat- egories, and the vehicle deck adding categories C and
 8

D to its capability - all in accordance with IMDG codes.

A diesel-electric machinery installation uses four
Wartsila 8L32 main engines driving ABB alternators to supply power to an ABB SAMI Megastar plant with four 2600kW propulsion motors geared to twin FP propellers. All electrical requirements are served from this system, including two Kamewa 1000kW bow thrusters.

Note: Illustration shows sister vessel Jauwrus

INCANTIERI moved into the commercial high-
 speed ferry market as a natural development of its naval work, introducing the steel-hulled Pegasus 7 in 1996 (Szgnificant Ships of 1996), built to the MDV (Monohull Deep Vee) 1200 design, where '1200' signi- fies the approximate displacement. The Swperseacat class - featured in the 1997 edition of this annual - with aluminium alloy hull followed. Now the series is continued by the first of four MDV 3000 Jupiter-class vessels, designed to operate between Civitavecchia (Rome) and Olbia in Sardinia, making up to six cross- ings each day.

Built with a 145m-long steel hull and aluminium superstructure, Avzes is understood to be the largest catr- carrying fast ferry in the world. There are six decks: three providing accommodation for 1800 passengers, and three - lower, main and hoistable upper garage - decks, with 2000 lane metres for 460 cars, or combina- tions such as 30 trailers or coaches, plus 100 cars. Pas- senger and vehicle access is through three ramp/doors in the flat stern, at main garage deck level.

The former enter over a centreline ramp into an enclosed vestibule with reversible escalators to the pas- senget decks. Vehicles use the 9m long ramps at the side entries, and these together with the wide beam, allow traffic to perform a ‘U-turn’ at the fore end to exit over the stern, so dispensing with bow doors. Internal ramps take vehicles to the lower garage, and to the upper hoistable deck which is formed of 26 hydraulically operated panels.

An alternative entry for passengers is at main deck level, through side doors amidships, adjacent to MES (marine evacuation system) positions capable of evac- uating all on board within 15 minutes. This deck accommodates 1208 tourist class, mainly seated in saloons fitted with blocks of three/four seats. Public spaces on this deck consist of a snack bar/lounge aft.
The upper passenger deck is for first-class travellers with open seating areas, and 584 reclining seats within saloons.

A number of alternative machinery installations are available for the MDV3000 design. That selected for
Aries uses four MTU 20V 1163 TB73 main engines
 10
 rated at 6500kW each, connected to the largest steering water jets yet built, through Maag twin-input/single- output reduction gearboxes, plus two General Electric 22,000kW gas turbines which drive the two inboard booster water jets, through two mote sets of Maag reduction gears.

This arrangement allows all six propulsive units to be used for 40knot speeds; four diesel engines and one tur- bine to give 31knots and four diesel engines alone provide 23knots. Four 820kW diesel-alternators are installed, and there are two 600kW bow thrusters, and two pairs of active computer-controlled fin stabilisers.

YUNDAI has experienced a long run of success with its design of circa-6000-unit car and truck carriers, now constructed in a building dock dedicated to this type of vessel, thus increasing productivity and the final quality of the product. Classed with both Det
Norske Veritas and the Korean Register, Assan Captain can catty a variety of motor vehicle types, loaded by roll-on/roll-off methods, as well as containers brought on board by trailer or container-carrying forklift trucks.
A total of 12 decks, including four which are hoistable, as well as tank top and open top deck, are available for vehicle transport, offering 53,750m?’ of stowage space. Transverse racking bulkheads have been dispensed with on No 5 (main/freeboard) deck, and this has provided much improved efficiency of loading and unloading, which is carried out over a stern quarter ramp/door, and over a height-adjustable side door for- watd of the engineroom on the main deck. Both these entries are arranged on the starboard side.

Within the vessel, six moveable ramps link the main deck with other decks, constructed so that they will be flush with the surrounding level when closed. All the access equipment has been supplied by Kvaerner and is designed to operate within heeling angles up to 3deg, and trim up to 2deg; audible and visual alarms indicat- ing these angles are provided at the stern and side ramps. A one-man control system is provided for each of the items except the manual lashing stoppers. In the cargo areas, support is provided by two rows of pillars installed at clear distances of approximately 13.4m in a longitudinal direction, and 11.4m transversely.

Hyundai built the MAN B&W 7S6OMC main engine which develops 19,460bhp MCR at 105rev/min, and three MAN B&W Holeby diesel-alternator sets each produce 1180kW at 720rev/min, including supply to a
Kawasaki bow thruster with an output of 1800hp. A
Becker flap rudder is also fitted. The cargo space is sub- divided into four groups for ventilation purposes, with
 12
 each receiving not less than 20 air changes hourly in port. Maximum fume concentrations during cargo han- dling comply with Australian Department of Health
Regulations 1975.

The hull is double-skinned up to deck 3, with the resultant side tanks linked to the outboard double-bot- tom tanks and used for water ballast. The central dou- ble-bottom tanks are divided by the centre girder and carry fuel. The design features a number of measures aimed at easy operation and reducing maintenance.
Notable are the use of GRP piping for the main ballast lines, a central cooling system for engineroom auxil- iaties, and low-pressure hydraulics for deck machinery.

OLITICAL events of recent years all but closed the
 once thriving Croatian (or Yugoslav as it was previ- ously) shipbuilding industry, but the re-establishment of relations with former Russian customers has brought about a revival, although Chinese operators have also helped - as witnessed by Iaaang from 3 Maj, presented in last year's edition of Szgnificant Ships. Russian con- tracts have been based mainly on a number of designs emanating from the Trogir shipyard, and Axzov Sea an example of an _ extensive 47,400dwt crude/ product/chemical tanker series ordered through the
Sovkomflot organisation.

This vessel has a cargo space within a double hull, containing five centrally divided cargo tanks with trans- verse and centreline bulkheads formed of troughed plate. Tank-side plating is stiffened within the double hull, and with deckhead stiffening arranged externally, a smooth internal surface for the Sigma Phenguard epoxy phenoline coating, as well as subsequent cleaning, is provided.

Five grades of oil or IMO Type III chemicals can be catried, and loaded or discharged simultaneously. ‘The latter operation uses 10 Frank Mohn hydraulically oper- ated submerged pumps - plus one portable unit, and can be completed in 16 hours. Loading time is 12 houts.

Other features of the cargo system include the pro- vision of 10 x 900kW Frank Mohn deck-mounted steam heaters serving the cargo tanks, and ‘cunifet' heating coils for the two slop tanks. Cargo piping is made of stainless steel. The inert-gas system has a capacity of 4650m’/hN, and a 100m°/h heater is pro- vided for COW and hot sea water tank washing, with one cleaning machine in each tank. The double-hull space is joined to the double bottom to provide com- mon, centrally divided water ballast tanks, and 1s tadiused at the bottom to form its connection with the tank top. |

Diesel Factory Brodosplit manufactured the MAN
B&W 6S50MC main engine. This develops 8310kW
MCR at 123rev/min, and when operating at 90% full power, provides a trial speed of 15.40knots. Electrical supply comes from three MaK/Uljanik-Siemens diesel- alternator sets. Steam is generated by an oil-fired and an exhaust-gas boiler. Crew complement is 12 officers
 14
 six berths allocated to a Suez crew.

BERGE ATLANTIC: Hyundai's first double-hull bulker

SNIPDUICEr: oo. eee Hyundai Heavy cecueaeeneueeeueeeneenens Industries Co Ltd, Korea
VESSEI'S NAME? 0... cc cece cece eee Berge Atlantic
HUI] NUMDEDS: oo... ccc eecceeeeeeeeeeeessueeneens 1094
Owner/operator: ............ Bergesen DY A/S, cee eeee ee eeeeeeee essa eteueesseseseaeeeaeseaeeanetanges Norway
DESIQNET: .......ceceeeeeeeeeeeeeeeees Hyundai Heavy cceeaeeueneeeeaeeneaeenens Industries Co Ltd, Korea sc (0 ere Norway (NIS)
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ...... ea, Nil
Total number of sister |

SNIPS Stil] ON OFC... cceeeeecceeeeseeesee eee eeen ees -

SMALL number of double-hull bulk carriers - that
 is, those true bulkers with hopper-shaped holds - have been with us for some years now, and the Panamax
Soldarnose class (Szgnifecant Ships of 1991) also the Top
Sugar presented elsewhere in this issue, are representa- tive. Nevertheless, Berge Atlantic is understood to be the first vessel of this type built by Hyundai, as well as probably being the largest. Owner and builder have fre- quently worked together in the past (such as Bergeland, Significant Ships of 1992), and this recent collaboration has been constructed against a long-term charter from
British Steel covering the import of iron ore for the UK steel-making industry.

As a percentage of this material comes from northern
Kurope and Canada, Det Norske Veritas Ice-1C requirements have been incorporated, with equipment designed to withstand temperatures of -30°C. This includes providing adequate air conditioning; heated windows with demisters; and heating of steering gear compartment, bosun's store and emergency generator room. Steam lines on deck are lagged for de-icing, and water ballast tanks kept free from ice by an air bubbling system, circulated from a screw-type 400m°*/h ait com- pressot.

Nine holds are laid out within the double hull, which is single-decked without forecastle. The side spaces are 6.5m wide, reducing to 2.65m in the double bottom, and together these form common water ballast tanks, separated at the centreline by a duct keel. Fuel is carried in side bunkers each side of the engineroom.

A good initial bending moment was incorporated at the design stage, giving plenty of flexibility for various loading conditions. In addition to LACS UR S18 scant- lings for transverse watertight bulkheads, and S21 requirements for hatch cover scantlings, the inner bot-

Le
 tom plating has been increased in thickness to cope with the effects of damage from 30tonne grabs and bulldoz- ers, and the classification notation covers sailing with only odd-numbered holds loaded with ore of SF12
FT3/LT designation.

The MacGregor hatch covets are of the weathertight two-panel side-rolling type, operated by hydraulic motor with rack-and-pinion mechanism and fitted with Mac-
Gregor's new-generation, solid-rubber FlexSeals for prolonged effectiveness. Those covers fitted to odd- numbered holds can be moved to either side to provide a ‘half-open' alternative. The steering gear is of the rotaty-vane type with three power units, one of which is a stand-by.

The MAN B&W 7S70MC main engine, built by
Hyundai, has an MCR of 26,740bhp/91rev.min, and an
NCR of 22,730bhp at 86.2rev/min. Bunker capacity provides for an endurance of 58 days, or approximately 21,200 sea miles steaming at a speed of 15.3knots.
Three MAN B&W Holeby diesel-alternator sets each have an output of 850kW, and two oil-fired boilers each produce 2500kg/h steam.

BOW MASTER: from Poland

Total number of sister

SHIOS Still ON OFeP: vo... cece cece ee tees eeeee ene 3

HILST feeder ships are common enough in many
 dry cargo trades, their use with oil transport is less so, but it is to service its fleet of ocean-going vessels, patticularly in the Far East, that Odjfell has ordered this fout-ship series. Within a double hull, these new-design ships (designated the B579 type) have a vertically troughed centreline bulkhead which, in conjunction with four horizontally troughed transverse bulkheads and two cofferdams formed of flat plate (enclosing Nos 6 and 7 tanks P&S), divides the space into 14 cargo tanks. These are constructed of Avesta Sheffield-type 316LN solid, acid-resistant stainless steel, designed for high-density cargoes and capable of withstanding high temperatures and vapour pressures.

Bow Master and her sisters meet the same high specifi- cation as larger vessels in the fleet and complement their services. Each tank is fitted with a Framo hydrauli- cally driven, 200m’/h submerged pump, separately con- nected to the manifold by stainless steel piping, and offering 14 cargo segregations within the IMO Interna- tional Bulk Chemical code for cargoes requiring ship
Types II and II. Refined petroleum cargoes and molasses can also be carried, and the tanks are indirectly heated by water, using stainless steel piping. Facilities ate also provided for tank washing with fresh/sea water, using programmable, multi-stage Scanjet machines.

An 800m?*/h nitrogen generator for inerting and padding purposes, and a 2500m’/h air dehumidification plant are fitted. Cargo operations are remotely con- trolled from a console in the wheelhouse using an
Autronica monitoting system, radar tank gauging and temperature, high-pressure and high-level alarms.

Polish engine builder H Cegielski constructed the
MAN B&W 6S35MC main engine, also the MAN
B&W Holeby 6L16/24 prime movers for the two A van Kaick 570kW diesel-alternator sets. The main engine develops 3600kW at 147rev/min and drives a
CP propeller for a service speed of 14.3knots, as well as an alternator producing 700kW, through a power take-off shaft from an ASUG gearbox running at 1800rev/min. Optimal manoeuvrability is obtained using a 400kW ABB Zamech bow thruster and a
Schilling high-lift rudder.

Illustration shows sister ship, with her charter name, P ¢» O
Nedlloyd Djibouti

HE reputation of German shipbuilders and own- ers for the innovative design of multipurpose cargo vessels, mainly built for charter to the large liner groups, is further enhanced by this new shallow- draught/short-leneth series from Flensburger, desig- nated the C-Box class. Like the Ecobox family of flex- ible cargo vessels introduced by the yard in 1994 (see
Trade Sol, Significant Ships of 1994), the C-Box displays the influence of shipowner Egon Oldendorff, a spe- cialist in these trades, which now owns the yard, and has taken most of the first deliveries into its fleet.
C-box is indeed a multi-purpose carrier, with five box-shaped, open holds without obstruction, within a double hull divided by double-skin transverse bulk- heads, which makes them suitable for paper cargoes.
Additionally, the holds are strengthened for heavy car- goes and grab discharge, and alternate hold loading is a feature. Dangerous cargoes of all IMO grades can be loaded in the mechanically ventilated No 2 hold, with some gtades in most of the other holds and on deck.
Grain stability regulations are satisfied, whilst uniform loadings of 21tonnes/m?’ on tank top, 3tonnes/m?* on deck, and 2tonnes/m* on hatches, plus a large deck space with flush fittings, makes the design friendly to project cargoes.
As a container carrier, 1300TEU can be accommo- dated, equivalent to 1O00TEU at 14tonnes load or 822 at 20tonnes. FEU loading is 637 (plus 26TEU), whilst
 28

A5ft, and the wide Euro/Bell units can also be carried.
Slim-line cell guides can be retrofitted and a heeling tank system using side ballast tanks is installed. Hatch covets ate MacGregor lift-away panel type, and two
MacGregor-Hagelund 60tonne cranes, capable of a 120tonne combined lift, are fitted on the port side, serving all container slots.

A Mitsubishi 6UEC5OLS II main engine - built at the designer's works in Japan - develops 11,220bhp MCR at 124rev/min for a service speed of around 17knots on a container design draught of 8m, and 16.7knots on a design load draught of 9m, with a corresponding eco- nomic fuel consumption of 29.4tonnes/day. Two alter- nators driven by Yanmar 900bhp diesel engines are fitted, with provision for a third if a supply to reefer boxes is required. The aft body hullform incorporates a skeg-type stern, and a Costa bulb is fitted to the rudder. The tall superstructure located right aft offers accommodation for 21 persons, including pilot and six-berth Suez crew - with certification for ship operation by 13 personnel.

CLANSMAN: ro-ro/passenger ferry for

Scottish Isles services

LANSMAWN is believed to be the first open-deck
 to-to passenger ferry to be built which complies with the requirements of the Stockholm Agreement of 1996, and a number of new measures, principally con- cerned with stability, had to be considered when formu- lating the design. Notably, freeboard to the vehicle deck has been increased in order to reduce the water-on-deck penalty, and freeing port areas made larger than was the case with earlier vessels. The imposition of these new rules on to a design whose dimensions were already restricted by conditions at the operating ports of Oban, Castlebay, Lochboisdale, Coll and Tiree, has necessitated a hullform of larger block co-efficient than might have been expected.

The through vehicle deck is covered, except for an area at the aft end, by an upper deck with tiered deck- houses. Access is ovet an articulated stern ramp with hinged finger and bridge flaps, and a bow ramp in three sections, fitted behind a watertight bow visor which is integral with the ship's structure. Up to 90 cars can be cattied on the vehicle deck, with an extra 10 on a hoistable mezzanine deck on the starboard side. All equipment is hydraulically operated.

Wing buoyancy spaces are provided at each side of the vehicle deck, and a central casing includes passenger accesses to the accommodation. Below this deck are the engineroom; main control room; compartment for the twin Ulstein 450kW bow thrusters; port and starboard stabilisers; fuel, water ballast and void spaces.

Accommodation is arranged for 634 passengers on the upper and boat decks, with the main entrance con- course amidships on the upper deck. The lounge bar is aft, and a TV lounge (starboard), lounge with semi- reclining chairs (port), and cafeteria with galleys and servery, forward. An observation lounge with panoramic views occupies the fore end of the boat deck, and includes a number of fully reclining chairs for overnight passengers. Crew members are housed aft of this lounge, with officers one deck higher, behind a full- width wheelhouse.

Twin Mak 8M32 main engines each developing 3840kW at 600rev/min, drive CP propellers at 270rev/min through Ulstein gearboxes for a service
 32
 speed of 16.5knots. A power take-off from each gear- box connects with a Stamford 1248kW alternator, and three Cummins/Stamfotd 352kW diesel-alternator sets are also fitted. These were packaged and supplied to the shipyard by Pelican Engineering, and are controlled through a basic power management system. Twin

Becker flap-type rudders are installed.

Lifesaving equipment satisfies MCA (UK Maritime &
Coastguard Agency) Class IIA rules and includes two 100-person totally enclosed lifeboats: a fast rescue boat, and a man overboard boat. A marine evacuation slide
 for 650 persons is fitted port and starboard.

STA RICAN STAR, pictured above whilst on trials in the colours of charterer Blue Star Line, is particularly notable as the 50th refrigerated cargo ves- sel to have been delivered by Shikoku. The machinery- aft configuration features four cargo holds - No 1 being accessed through the extended forecastle, with three intermediate decks dividing the cargo spaces into 16 independent compartments with two air coolers each, and eight cooling sections. These are served by four sets of refrigerating plant with brine chiller units, housed in a machinery room on the upper deck, for- ward of the main deckhouse.

These are capable of providing conditions within the range -25°C to +15°C, suitable for the carriage of fresh and frozen products, with a homogeneous airflow and the required temperature maintained by means of a computerised control system. A controlled atmosphere notation for eight zones has been assigned by the clas- sification society, and the plant is suitable for conver- sion to a more environmentally friendly refrigerant in the future.

Rasy access to the holds is provided through large (for this class of vessel) hatches, closed by hydraulical- ly operated MacGregor folding covers, and served by four IHI high-speed, hydraulic cylinder-luffing deck cranes: two trated for 8tonne lifts at 70m/min, and two
Operating at 36tonne/16m/min, alternatively 8tonnes at 70m/min. Costa Rican Star is equipped to carry a total of 258TERU, including 80 reefer units, in the holds beneath hatch openings, and on deck and hatch covers.
 36

In common with many refrigerated cargo carfiers, cars (total 531) can be loaded below decks as a backhaul cargo.

The main engine is a Mitsui-built MAN B&W 6L6O0MC unit, developing 15,600bhp at 123rev/min to drive a FP propeller for a service speed of 20.3knots, making use of a newly developed hull-form. Electrical power is derived from four Daihatsu/Nishishiba 1000kVA alternators, with steam produced by a com- posite boiler.

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF1998
DAWN MERCHANT: fast ro-pax ferry for
 new service
 shipbuilder: ....... Astilleros de Sevilla SRL, Lecce eee e cess eeeeeeeueseaueesseeeeeeuetuneteneeenentieess Spain
Vessel’S Name? ......ceceeeeee. Dawn Merchant
FUL] NUMDES: oo. ccc eee cee eeceeeceeeeeeaeeens 287
Owner/operator: .Cenargo International Ltd, eee eeu eeeueeeeseeeceuecuueeeceuueueeeeueeeasevaeeueetnentns UK
Designer:...Astilleros de Sevilla SRL, Spain ic 0 re UK
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ........, . Nil
Total number of sister
 ships still on order: ........... 3 plus 2 options

AANOMBER of contracts were placed with Spanish state-owned shipbuilder Astilleros Espafioles fol- lowing its decision to pursue work in the ferry sector; however, the Government's recent de-nationalisation plans have meant that some of this work has been com- pleted by companies now no longer part of the AESA group (see Ararere, page 8). Others have been completed by new companies formed within a re-organised group, and it is the former Seville yard, now trading as Astilleros de Sevilla, which has delivered the first of four fast ves- sels to Cenargo, for service on a new Liverpool-Dublin fo-pax service (see also Skane, page 103).

Vehicles enter Dawn Merchant over the stern at main deck level at Liverpool, using a 15m total length door/ramp with 17m wide driveway, capable of carry-
Ing two 45tonne trailers. Unloading at Dublin is through side-hingeing bow doors with the two leaves opening outwards, over a three-section bow-doot ramp 17m total length x 4.5m wide, suitable for a load of one 45tonne trailer.

Section 1 of this ramp forms an internal watertight door when stowed, with the other sections connected when it is used as a ramp. Two hoistable tilting ramps, which can be lowered from either end, link the main and upper decks to give a total of 2000m lane length on the two vehicle decks, where clear height is 5.20m. Each ramp is completely enclosed within a casing which has flood control doors at either end in accordance with the
Stockholm Agreement, whose requirements for higher sutvivabilty standards have been complied with throughout the design.

Below the main deck there are 11 compartments, with voids at the hull side and fuel and water tanks at the cen- tre to meet safety criteria, together with anti-rolling and heeling tanks, twin machinery rooms and an auxiliary engineroom. Four main engines are fitted: Wartsila 9L38 units each developing 5940kW at 600rev/min, and arranged in pairs to drive two Wartsila Wichmann CP propellers through twin-input/single-output de Schelde gears with Vulkan couplings.

Rach gearbox also has two power take-offs - for a 1400kW alternator and another for a 640kW alternator - to provide electrical power, including supply to 40 reefer sockets. They are supplemented by the output from two
Wartsila-Indar 880kW diesel-powered sets. Two Brunvoll 1300kW bow thrusters are fitted and the twin rudders are of the Becker high-lift flap type.

Crew accommodation for a complement of 48 is
 38
 arranged on decks 8 and 9, with 30 outside/double and 27 inside/double cabins on deck 8 available for 114 pas- sengers. Deck 7 is the location of a reception/informa- tion centre; duty-free/gift shop; cafeteria restaurant seating 100 persons; 100-seater bar lounge and a TV viewing atea. /\ video lounge accommodates 20 persons on deck 8, where there is also a lounge fitted with 96 reclining seats.

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF1998
DEEPWATER PATHFINDER: state-of-the-art deepwater drill ship
 shipbuilder: ....Samsung Heavy Industries vucueeeseeeeeeeeeueeeeueaceneeeeeeereaeentess Co Ltd, Korea
Vessel’S name.......... Deepwater Pathfinder
HUI] NUMDE| SL oo. ecccceeeceeeeeeeeeenens 1220
Owner/operator:....Deepwater Drilling LLC (Conoco/Reading & Bates joint venture)/ ccc eeeeeeueueeueueueaeeeeneuseeueeaeneenen Conoco, USA
Designer:........ Samsung Heavy Industries occ ueueueueeeeeceeeeeueeeteetuseeeteeneneneas Co Ltd, Korea nc 0 rere Panama
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ww... eee Nil
Total number of sister

ShiIPS Still ON Oder: wo. ec ececeeeeeeeeeeees 2

Tee concentrated effort by Samsung to enter the matket for specialist offshore vessels has already been rewarded with contracts for FPSO unit and shut- tle tankers (such as Kometik, Significant Ships of 1997).
Today, this strategy continues with what are understood to be the first ultra-deep-water drill ships, so described because of their designed ability to work at 2286m water depths in the Gulf of Mexico, with a potential to operate down to 3048m.

Deepwater Pathfinder has been built with a double hull which complies with OPA-90 requirements, and is sub- divided by two longitudinal bulkheads and transverse divisions to form a series of holds/tanks suitable for water ballast, oil, drilling water, mud, cement, barytes and bentonite. Equipment is also provided for an extended well test system (EWT), including 100,000bbl oil storage in which treatment of well fluid can be car- tied out in environmentally sound conditions.
Simultaneous drilling and testing can also be main- tained.

Also within the hull is a 12.8m x 12.48m moonpool, through which the 52m tall derrick operates; port, starboard and centreline compartments, forward and aft (total six), for 4000kW ABB/Kamewa azimuthing thrusters; a forward auxiliary machinery room; and pott, starboard and centre main enginerooms aft, which house the diesel-electric power plant. The lat- ter comprises 3 x 7OOOkKW and 3 x 4700kW
Wartsila/ABB alternator sets (Geislinger elastic damping couplings are fitted to each) supplying power to the thrusters.

A high degree of redundancy has been incorporated
 40
 into the design, particularly with regard to the dynamic positioning system, for which American Bureau of
Shipping's DPS-3 notation - indicating triple redundan- cy - has been awarded to a drill ship for the first time.
Combined with satellite and seabed positioning sys- tems, this ensures that the vessel can maintain station and drill continuously in sea states up to 10-year Gulf of Mexico storm level.

Hotel-standard accommodation for 130 persons is provided in a ‘low-noise’ superstructure built on the forecastle, and surmounted by a helicopter deck.
Extensive deck space available on the upper deck has been utilised by houses containing workshops and electrical equipment, with pipe racks above. Aft of the derrick, stowage is arranged for risers, and the open deck provides a location for rented well test equipment and other gear. Three gantry-type cranes of 25tonnes, 50tonnes and 320tonnes respectively, serve the storage areas, and there are also three pedestal cranes of 50tonnes SWL each, and one of 85tonnes.

HEN Disney Cruise Line ordered Desney Magic
 and her sister Dasney Wonder, the intention was to bring back, as far as was possible, the traditional exter- nal shape and elegance of the pre/post-war classic lin- ers, and the accompanying photograph shows that this has certainly been achieved. The entire concept demon- strates typical Disney innovation. For example, it is pos- sible for passengers to check their luggage in at theit local airport and next find it in their cabin on board ship in America - and again waiting at Orlando Airport for the flight home.

Originality continues throughout the seven-day vaca- tion package offered by Disney. This involves a three- or four-day cruise from Port Canaveral, followed by a four- or three-day stay at the Walt Disney World Resort on the company’s private Bahamian island, Castaway
Cay. Arrangements, both on the island and aboard ship, are based upon providing completely separate activities for the categories of passenger carried: adults, families and children, all fashioned with typical Disney flamboy- ance and attention to detailed organisation.

One deck on Désney Magic is solely for children, pro- viding an area of 1350m° for their fully-supervised entertainment. Teenagers have their own club in one of the funnels, with virtual reality and computer centres.
Adults have sole use of a pool, spa and fitness areas, and within the forward funnel a multi-screen sports club which broadcasts worldwide sports news and events. They also have a separate restaurant for their own use, but the main eating experience is provided from three themed restaurants offering ‘rotational din- ing’, where diners, table companions and waiters rotate en-bloc each night between the three venues. Two the- atres, one of which seats 975 and is three-decks high, ate the other major entertainment centres for the 2400 maximum passengers normally carried in 879 cabins, 73% of which are ‘outside’, with 41% of that total hav- ing a balcony.

Power supply is derived from a diesel-electric instal-

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 lation which uses five GMT-Sulzer 16ZAV40S diesel engines as ptime-movers for five 11,200kW Ansaldo
GSCR 11Y14, alternators. A Geislinger elastic damp- ing coupling is fitted between each engine and its alter- natot. Each engine develops 11,520kW at 514rev/min and, with its alternator, is installed three/two in two enginerooms separated from each other by a compatt- ment containing two 19,000kW General Electric
Canada propulsion motors. Shaft lines from these units pass through the aftermost engineroom to the twin FP propellers. Two hinged-flap rudders are fitted and there are three Fincantieri 1800kW thrusters for- ward and two aft. Two Aalborg 10,000ke/h boilers are
 the main source of steam production.

Illustration shows sister ship Dongbang Glory

AY interesting adaptation of the ubiquitous ro-ro design to satisfy the requirements of a specialised trade is demonstrated by the four ships in this series, built by Dae Sun for transporting hot-rolled steel coils in
Korean coastal waters. This trading area has also neces- sitated a shallow-draught hullform to be developed.

Central to the design is the increasing use by Korean steel manufacturers of special cassette pallets for carry- ing coils under conditions which provide protection against weather and transit damage, at all stages of the opetation. Each cassette can carry 60tonnes and is brought into the vessel over a 9.36m x 19.96m stern ramp/door supplied by Seo Hae Marine Systems, for stowage on the single cargo deck where 532m of lane space is available to accommodate 76 cassettes. An interesting comparison can perhaps be made with the
Japanese-designed and built Hakuryw Maru, presented in the 1991 edition of Szgnificant Ships.

To guatd against movement of the cargo during a voyage (and thereby possible damage), 18 sets of cas- sette locking devices, operated by hydraulic cylinder, have also been supplied by Seo Hae, and a 176m’ roll- damping tank has been fitted on the upper deck for- watd of midships. An anti-heeling system using two 285m? side ballast tanks, and trimming tanks forward and aft, maintain level conditions during loading and discharge.

The two-decked hull is without sheer but has a 0.25m straight-line camber. The vehicle deck is 6.75m above base, and below this level there is a 4.00m wide double skin P&S forming water ballast tanks, and a central void space containing supporting structure for the vehicle deck. This is divided into P&S sections by a central casing which provides fore and aft access, and houses engine exhausts.

Twin Hyundai-built MAN B&W 6132/40 main

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 engines each develop 2640kW MCR at 750rev/min, driving a pair of FP propellers through Niigata gear- boxes for a service speed of 14.50knots, at 85% full power and with a sea margin of 15%. Iawasaki thrusters ate fitted at bow and stern, and electrical power is derived from three 550kW Yanmat/ Hyundai diesel-alternator sets.

LATION is the seventh Fantasy-class cruise liner to
 be built for Carnival by Kvaerner, continuing a remarkable series begun with Fantasy in 1990 (see Szgnif- icant Ships of 1990), and now completed with the deliv- ety in October 1998 of an eighth vessel, Paradise.
Believed to be the longest series of cruise-liner sisters yet built, these vessels share the same principal dimen- sions and are generally similar in outward appearance - notably all carrying the Carnival trademark winged fun- nel - but each has its own individual themed interior which, with E/aton, is "The creative spirit of the Mytho- logical Muses’.

This identity is applied throughout decks 4 to 7, occu- pied by 1020 passenger cabins with a total of 2634 beds, and on decks 8 to 13, the location of the main public spaces. A six-deck-high atrium surmounted by a glass dome has deck 7 as its lower level, and features panoramic elevators. Deck 8 presents the two main din- ing rooms and a 1000-seat show lounge spread over two decks. Entertainment facilities popular on every cruise liner, such as a night club, numerous bars, cafes, card room, library, shops, fitness centre, casino and rubber- decked jogging track, are all provided. In addition, E/- tion is the first Carnival vessel to have a conference suite, whilst "Virtual World’, an electronic games playground for adults and children alike, is likely to prove a popular attraction.

Where E/aton and Paradise do differ significantly from their earlier sisters is in their machinery installation. Fan- tasy was the first modern newbuilding cruise liner to adopt a diesel-electric power station system for propul- sion and electrical supply, and this popular concept is now taken a stage further. The new sisters are the first cruise liners to be fitted with azimuthing Azipod propulsion, whereby the electric motors are inside hubs, thus dispensing with bevel gears and Z-drives, also rud- ders. The basic design of the Fan/asy-class diesel-electric installation is retained, but with the two propulsion

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 motots now fitted outside the ship, in propeller pods under the stern.

Six Wartsila-NSD 12V38 (7920kW/600rev/min) main engines (as opposed to Sulzer Z-type machinery on all earlier ships) are prime movers for six ABB 11,000kVA alternators. Fitted in two enginerooms, these supply a 6.6kV bus-bar from which the main hotel services and propulsion motors are powered. The latter each develop 14,000kW and in this new arrange- ment arte housed in Azipods suspended under the stern.

These can azimuth through 360deg and, unlike con- ventional propellers, pw// the vessel, rather than push it through the water. The system has been engineered and supplied by ABB Marine (although development was jointly with Kvaerner Masa) and is controlled by cyclo- converters of ABB manufacture, which provide a speed range between 0 and 146rev/min.

The Azipod system includes amongst its benefits the elimination of shaft lines, and improved passenger comfort by greatly reducing engine noise and vibra- tion. Space and weight on board have been saved, and steering gear, rudders, and three tunnel-type stern thrusters have all been deleted from the original Fan- tasy specification. Trials results indicate an 8% improvement in propulsive efficiency; a 40tonnes weekly saving in fuel consumption; 30% reduction in turning circle at full speed; and retention of steerage during a crash stop.

ITH the contract for this vessel, the EISA ship-
 yatd returned to the construction of Panamax bulk carriers after a period building sophisticated product tankers and container vessels. The concept chosen follows the traditional bulk carrier format, with single hull, single deck and no forecastle, although the upper deck forward does have a substantial sheer. Top and bottom wing tanks are fitted within the holds, forming water ballast spaces: the lower tanks being joined with double-bottom tanks divided by a centre- line duct keel.

There are seven cargo holds, closed by hydraulically operated, side-rolling Kvaerner hatch covers. Each con- sists of two panels and provides a clear opening 16m wide x 14.4m long, with the possibility for opening only one panel if required. The ship is suitable for the car- riage of ore, coal, grain and other bulk cargoes, and is strenethened for heavy cargoes and grab discharge.

The recommendations of the IMO Code for the
Carriage of Bulk Cargoes have been followed, partic- ularly those relating to coal, and no fuel spaces have been arranged under the holds - side tanks in the engineroom being used instead. A cofferdam is fitted between the engineroom and No 7 hold to prevent cargo. heating, and the bilge system for the holds has been separated from that serving the machinery space.

The transverse corrugated bulkheads between holds have been constructed on stools, and the design allows for the vessel to sail loaded but with Nos 2, 4 and 6 holds empty. Alternatively, No 4 hold only may be left empty, and in addition, this hold can be flooded on bal- last voyages. Approximately 11.6% of high-tensile steel was used in the construction with grade AH-36 adopted for the main deck structure. An advanced epoxy paint system has been applied throughout the vessel, and especially within the ballast tanks, where careful grinding of sharp edges and welds was carried

SO
 out prior to painting.

The machinery installation is centred upon a Hyundai- built Sulzer 6ORTA62U main engine which develops 12,240bhp at 90rev/min. This is directly coupled to a FP propeller and, at 90% MCR and allowing 15% sea mar- gin, gives a service speed of 14.5knots. Electrical supply is derived from three 590kW Sulzer/Hyundai(Heeco)-
Siemens diesel-alternators, and steam is generated from an oil-fired and an exhaust-gas boiler. A central cooling system using two-speed pumps controlled by computer is fitted, and the complete installation is masterminded and monitored by a computer network with terminals installed in the engineroom and accommodation.

RONT CENTURY its the first of five C-class

VLCCs ordered by Frontline from Hyundai, and constructed to a design which incorporates energy-saving and marine environmental protection features within a new hullform, which has a bluff forebody and wide (58m) beam. Much emphasis has been placed on the structural design in order to reduce hull deflections - which place high stress on secondary membets, and a range of detailed structural analyses has been performed.
These include fore end tank structure and secondary members’ fine mesh analyses; examination of vibration throughout the entire ship range; also bow impact and fatioue strength.

Three of the vessels are being built to Det Norske
Veritas rules, where they satisfy the requirements of the
CSA-1 structural streneth and fatique strength notation, and two to Lloyd's Register, with the inclusion of the
ShipRight PC-based advanced procedures for design, construction and life-time ship care.

The single-decked hull has no forecastle, and within a double skin it is divided into 15 cargo tanks, plus two for slops which are coated with coal tar epoxy. The side spaces ate combined with centrally divided double-bot- tom tanks to form water ballast compartments which, with the pumproom, are continuously monitored for hydrocarbon gases. The cargo pumping system uses three Shinko steam turbine pumps which together pro- vide a maximum unloading rate of 15,000m’/h, whilst a loading rate of 20,500m°/h is possible through the cargo manifolds.

A Super Cargo cargo control system allows one-man operation of loading and discharging duties, including safety and monitoring functions. Another suppott sys- tem incorporated is Super Plant, designed to detect dif- ficulties in the main engine, alternator prime movers and other machinery, using artificial intelligence and sophisticated data acquisition techniques.

The main engine is a Hyundai-MAN B&W
TS8OMC(VI) unit with a maximum output of 34,650bhp at 79rev/min. Service rating is 92% MCR (31,920bhp at 76.9rev/min), which enables a service
 52
 speed of 15.7knots to be attained using a 9700mm fout- bladed propeller.

A complement of 33 is housed in the six-tier deck- house aft, above which is a wheelhouse equipped with an advanced integrated navigation system carrying out route planning, collision manoeuvring, grounding avoidance and navigation monitoring,

HANEBURG: compact 800TEU Turkish- design container ship
 shipbuilder: ...... Marmara Shipyard, Turkey
VESSEIS NAME? oo... eceece cece eeeee eee Haneburg
HUI] NUMDES: ....ccceece cece eee eeueeee eee eene enn 057
Owner/OPeratOr: .... ccc ccccceeccseecseeeseeeseeeen eens
Leveeeees Phoenix Reederei GmbH, Germany
Designefr:.......... Marmara Shipyard, Turkey
FLAG! ..cccecceececeeeeceseeusesueseeueeeaeeeueeens Germany
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ........... eee, Nil
Total number of sister

SNIPS Stil] ON OFCel: oo... ccc cee eecee cece cece eeeens T

ANEBURG is the first delivery of Marmara's in-
 house design Marcon 800 series. This follows on from the smaller Marcon 400 type, and has been devel- oped by the yard to optimise the carriage of most sizes of container (especially with the aid of movable cell ouides), and the speed and efficiency with which they can be handled. The double-skin hull has an extended forecastle, and is divided into four holds, sized to suit 40ft containers as follows: Nos 1 and 2 are one con- tainer-length long, and numbers 3 and 4, two contain- ers long.

In addition, a maximum of six rows can be accom- modated in the hold, and Nos 2, 3 and 4 holds can take 9ft Gin and 8ft 6in high boxes. Non-standard 30ft to 49ft containers can also be loaded, and maximum stack loads are 122tonnes in holds, 50tonnes on hatch covers and 94tonnes on deck. The trading area of the design is enhanced by the application of the Germanischer Lloyd
E-class. ice strengthening notation.

Electrical connections are available for 36 refrigerated containers in the holds and 60 on deck, with their con- dition monitored from the bridge. Requirements for the catriage of dangerous goods are incorporated, with fresh ait supply for the reefer boxes and ventilation for the dangerous goods maintained by hold fans providing 18 air changes hourly. A total of 803TEU may be car- tied - 500 when homogeneously loaded with 14tonnes.
This includes stowage on the upper deck, above the machinery room.

Movable cell guides are fitted in all holds, and per- manent stools for container stowage built into the structure at the ship's side. Cargo handling is facilitated by two NMF 40tonne deck cranes built into the port side. Stability whilst loading and unloading is main- tained using an Intering 800tonne/min anti-heel sys- tem. Hydraulically operated folding covers, made locally by Mariner Ships Equipment, are fitted to each hatch.

Propulsive power is derived from an MAN B&W 9140/54 main engine which develops 6480kW at 550rev/min, and drives a CP propeller through a Renk gearbox for a speed at full power of 17knots. A power take-off drives a 1462kVA alternator, and there are also three Deutz/van Kaick diesel-driven sets each producing 625kVA. Two thermal-oil boilers - one fuelled by exhaust gas - satisfy steam demands.
Assisted by a 450kW bow thruster, the Marcon 800
 60
 design is highly manoeuvrable, with steering and machinery control handled from the wheelhouse and bridge wings.

Accommodation for a complement of 20 is provided right aft in a seven-tier deckhouse built on the poop. A tall casing stands independent of the main structure at the aft, port corner of the deckhouse and isolates the cabins from the noise and heat of the machinery uptakes.

JOHANN SCHU
 enipulider: Lec eeeeeaeesseees Jiangnan Shipyard ceeueeuuetueeeueeeueeaeeuuess (Group) Co Ltd, China

Cee oldest shipyard began development work on LPG tanker designs in conjunction with MARIC, a decade ago. The yard's only previous completion of this type, however, was a small vessel for a domestic owner, so this breakthrough with a two-ship order for a specialist overseas company is of considerable signifi- cance. It has been marked by the opening of a new fab- tication shop (already being increased in size to cope with later contracts), specifically to build the technically advanced tanks required for this class of vessel.

For Johann Schulte, this workshop was used to fabricate three free-standing bi-lobe tanks with a total geometric volume of 16,500m’. These are supported within a single- skin hull, in each of three holds having upper wing tanks used for water ballast and washing water, with one port side compartment serving as a sewage holding tank. Bot- tom wing tanks, joined with double-bottom tanks sepa- rated at the centreline by a duct keel, carry water ballast.
Cargoes carried are within the Type 2G range with maxi- mum density of 0.972tonnes/m*, such as anhydrous ammonia, butadiene, butane-propane mixture, ethylene oxide(EO)/prophlene oxide mixtures with maximum EO content of 30% by weight, propane, propylene, proph- lene oxide, and VCM. Two auxiliary cylindrical gas tanks are fitted on deck forward of the compressor house.

A semi-tefrigerated system can handle three compati- ble cargo grades simultaneously, and includes two com- plete reliquefaction units and one cargo compressor, housed in a deckhouse on the upper deck. Six 250m*/h
Svanehoj DW150/150-3-K+1 electric deepwell pumps, and two NMB150e booster pumps with a capacity of 450m’*/h are fitted: other equipment provided includes a steam heated cargo heater/vaporiser, a gas detection plant with 15 sample points, an inert-gas system produc- ing 3600m°/h and a high-pressure oil system for quick closing of valves.

The machinery installation includes a Shanghai Ship-
 62
 yatd-built Sulzer 6ORTA52U main engine with an MCR of 9360kW at 135rev/min. A 1080kW shaft-driven alternator and three Yanmar/Taiyo 800kW diesel-alter- nators ate fitted, and there are two vertical, oil-fired boil- ers and an exhaust-gas economiser producing steam. A 750kW Kamewa thruster is installed at the bow.

KENNICOT: serving the Alaska Marine Highway
 shipbuilder:............ Halter Marine Inc, USA
VESSEI'S NAME? oo... cc cecce cece cece eee eeee es Kennicot
FU] NUMDES: 00. cccccecc cece eee eeeeee eee es 1510

SNIPS Still ON OFC: oo... ccce cece eeee eens Nil

HE Alaska Marine Highway System operates a fleet
 of nine vessels, serving the needs of American coastal communities above and including the Canadian port of Prince Rupert, and Kennicot was designed with the help of input from residents along this isolated coastline. This interesting vessel can perform a triple function: when operating as a day-cruise ferry, 748 pas- sengers can be carried on various south-east Alaska routes, and to Bellingham (Washington), the southern terminal on the USA/Canada border, and Prince
Rupert. By night, 500 passengers can be carried on routes including open ocean crossings such as the Gulf of Alaska, reaching as far as Dutch Harbour in the
Aleutian Islands.

An unusual third function is to act as an emergency command post in case of earthquakes, oil spills or similar disasters, for which purpose an eight-channel satellite communications system and additional VHF and UHF capabilities have been installed. When serv- ing in this mode, the forward passenger lounge is taken over as a unified response centre. A helicopter deck for use by the US Coast Guard ts fitted above the aft accommodation, and a 5tonne emergency response crane can be mounted aft. A portable floating dock, stowed under the vehicle deck forward when not in use, can be deployed over the stern for use by small vessels, and decontamination showers are atranged below the vehicle deck.

A total of 314 berths are available in 109 cabins, spread over first and tourist classes, where private toi- lets are provided, and in simple two-berth 'roomettes'.
Five cabins are designated for disabled passenger use.
Public areas include a dining saloon, cafeteria, bars and viewing lounges. A solarium, open at its aft end and featuring overhead windows, occupies much of the wheelhouse deck.

A mix of 39 cars and 20 x 40ft trailers, or 120 cars, can be carried on the vehicle (main) deck, with access over the stern, or through side doors in the aft quarter
 64

P&S. Existing ro-ro facilities at ports visited can be accommodated by using the side entries and driving on to a 40tonne vehicle elevator, closed at the sides by roller curtain doors. A turntable is arranged at the aft end of the vehicle deck to assist with positioning cars and trailers as required.

The machinery installation is based upon two Wart- sila 12V32LNE main engines, each providing 4918kW at 750rev/min to give a service speed of 16.75knots and a maximum of 19knots. The drive to a pair of Lips
CP propellers is through Lufkin gearboxes, each of which is connected by power take-off to a 2400kW alternator. Two Caterpillar diesel-driven alternator sets, which each develop 1440kW, complete the electrical supply, and a 1679kW Elliot-White-Gill bow thruster is fitted.

LOCH RANNOCH: technically - advanced shuttle tanker

Shipbuilder:. Daewoo Heavy Industries Ltd, cee ceee eee eeue eeu eeue eee eee ses een eea see tenseeneeneeanenaes Korea
VeESSEl'S NAMEL.... cece eeeees Loch Rannoch
Hull NUMDES: 0... cece cece eee eeee esse nese eens 5109
Owner/operatOr:............c006. Maersk Co Ltd/ Le cccvueeueseueeuueeueeeneseesenes BP Shipping Ltd, UK
Designer:... Daewoo Heavy Industries Ltd, errr Korea i a 0 re UK
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ...........c cee Nil
Total number of sister

Ships Still ON OFder: oo... eeteeeeeeee eee: Nil

ESIGNED to service the Schiehallion oil field
 west of Shetland, Loch Rannoch is operated by BP
Shipping on a seven-year charter from disponent ownet Maersk Shipping, delivering crude oil from the
Harland & Wolff, purpose-built FPSO, (see page 93) to
Sullom Voe tefinery on a four/five-day cycle. As this routine involves frequent berthing, flat-side scantlings have been increased and a glass-flake coating applied, with deck areas in way of mooring equipment protect- ed by anti-abrasive products.

Operating conditions have greatly influenced the design criteria, which are based upon anticipated 6m wave heights as against 4.5m in the North Sea, and a predominantly north-westerly swell. As a result, the bow loading system has been specially designed, and the forecastle height increased 3m above normal practice. , 
Scantlings have been determined following slam- ming, fatigue and sloshing analyses, and are based on a draught of 16m (SLWL is 15.6m), with a 10% margin applied to hull girder strength. Grade D steel is used for deck and side shell, and the entire structure has a high safety margin and comprehensive anti-corrosion protection. A Strainstall hull stress monitoring system with eight gauges is fitted.

Within a double-hull which extends aft to include the fuel tanks, there are two longitudinal bulkheads (con- tinued into the machinery spaces to provide structural continuity) forming, with transverse divisions, 18 cargo and two slop tanks. The piping system allows two etades of cargo to be handled using two 5500m*/h pumps, with ballasting carried out through FRP piping and two 2300m’/h pumps.

Bow loading utilises a Hitec Marine coupling, trans- ferring oil from the FPSO at 9000m°*/h, and incor- potating a vapour returns system whereby volatile
 66
 organic compounds can be piped back to the FPSO to reduce their emission into the atmosphere. The make-up of Schiehallion crude requires cargo heating to be provided in the tanks up to 40°C, and fixed inert gas, COW and tank cleaning systems are also fit- ted.

A full-redundancy propulsion concept has been introduced, including fitting twin MAN B&W 7SS5OMC main engines in separate compartments, with fuel treatment and purification equipment occu- pying a third area. Total output is 27,160bhp at 127rev/min, driving twin CP propellers for a service speed of 14.29knots at 78% MCR and with a 20% sea margin. Electrical requirements are satisfied by four 2800kW alternators, and manoeuvring and station keeping, in conjunction with a Cegelec DPS 902 dynamic positioning system, are enhanced by fitting
Ulstein thrusters: 2 x 2500kW units forward and 2 x 800kW ones aft, plus two Schilling Monovec high-lift rudders.

TR: trio of chemical/products tankers, built at
Daedong's new Chinhae 'greenfield' yard for a
Vladivostock-based owner, will be followed by a num- bet of similar vessels for other operators. Suitable for wotldwide trading, they have double hulls with small top and bottom wing tanks, within which six-pairs of catgo tanks and two slop tanks are arranged. ‘The cen- treline and transverse bulkheads are troughed and built on stools, and the smooth surface which they, and the inside bulkhead of the double skin presents, is main- tained at the deckhead, where strength is built-in exter- nally through a combination of longitudinal beams and deep transverse girders.

For most of its length above the cargo spaces, the upper deck has been given a significant straight-line camber, running from its junction with the upper wing tanks P&S. The entire side-tank configuration is joined
 68
 with the double-bottom tanks - centrally divided by a duct keel, to form water ballast spaces. Moska/vo is designed primarily to carry crude oil, oil products and
IMO Grade III cargoes, with tanks 2, 4 and 6 suitable for those of specific gravity 1.53.

Some 150 commodities, including caustic soda and benzine, can additionally be loaded into the tanks which ate coated with phenolic epoxy (ballast tank coating is tat epoxy). Cargo piping is grade SUS316L stainless steel, and unloading is carried out by means of an hydraulically operated, 600m°/h submerged pump in each tank, with two similar 300m’/h pumps serving the slop tanks.

The main engine has been built by Hyundai and is a
MAN B&W 6S50MC-VI unit developing 10,150bhp full power at 117rev/min. Three Yanmar/Taiyo diesel- alternator sets produce 600kW each, and two oil-fired water tube boilers supply steam. Control can be effected from either engine control room or wheelhouse using a
Norcontrol AutoChief IV system. Accommodation is provided for a complement of 15 officers and 10 crew, plus six Suez crew in the aft superstructure, with life- saving handled by an over-the-stern launched, free-fall
 lifeboat.

AVION BRITANNIA begins a new series of

North Sea shuttle tankers ordered from the
Astilleros Espanoles Group (AESA) by Norwegian owners. The design closely follows that of EZsabeth
Knutsen, delivered in 1997 and described in Significant
Ships of that year; however, the internal tank arrange- ment is different, with Navion opting to have two lon- eitudinal bulkheads within a double-hull format.

In conjunction with six transverse divisions this min- imises surface movement and sloshing of the cargo - these being the major causes of hydrocarbon vapour emission whilst loading - and provides 18 cargo tanks and two slop tanks. The double hull structure forms side and bottom tanks used for water ballast, and also encompasses the fuel tanks in the engineroom.

Cargo can be loaded either through the midships man- ifold, or by means of a bow loading system using a com- bined mooring and hose connection on the forecastle. A third method employs submerged turret loading from a buoy anchored to the sea bed and connected by pipeline to the production facility. This mates with a cone in the ship's bottom through which oil is pumped. The cone is located in a trunk abaft the fore peak which is open to the sea, and any leakages are contained here prior to transfer to the slop tanks. |

Two segtegated grades of crude oil can be handled using four Kvaerner 3000m’/h pumps, with operations controlled and monitored from the bridge. A tank radar sounding system measures the ullage in the 20 tanks, and inert-gas pressures and temperatures at three levels are also recorded. Loading and discharging trates are 8000m’/h for the bow and turret loading systems, and 12,000m?/h through the manifold. Two suction/dis- charge lines, aluminium heating coils in the bottoms of cargo and slop tanks, two crude oil washing lines, and a 15,000m°/h inert gas plant are all fitted. A ballast gaug- ing system serves 17 tanks, and draught sensors are installed.

A fully redundant machinery installation has two
Manises-MAN B&W 6LGO0MC main engines located in separate compartments with independent control rooms. Each engine develops 11,520kW MCR at 123rev/min and drives a CP propeller for a service speed, at 90% MCR, of 15knots. The required high degree of manoeuvrability and position-keeping is sat- isfied by these propellers; also by two 2200kW thrusters at the bow, and one of 1400kW aft - all extendable to
 70
 form azimuthing units for positioning; and two high- performance rudders, all linked to a comprehensive dynamic positioning system meeting Det Norske Veri- tas' 'Watch One' notation. The vessel can be moored at the offshore facility in up to 4.5m significant wave heights and 25/30 knots wind speed, with position maintained in up to 5.5m wave heights and 30/35knot wind speed.

Electrical power supplies come from _ two
Wartsila/Alconza 3200kW, and two 1600kW diesel- alternators, controlled from a switchboard divided into port and starboard sections and located in each engine control room.

P&O NEDLLOYD SOUTHAMPTON: leading the
Far East trade challenge
 shipbuilder: ...lshikawajima-Harima Heavy edecueeuseeeueeeuseuesuess Industries Co Ltd, Japan
 veeeee eee eeeeeeeans P&O Nedlloyd Southampton
Hull NUMDES: oo. ccc ccceccseeeseeecee essen eens 3088

FAQ! Lec cecccecccsecesseeseeeeueeesseeeeueesuuesesseesaness UK

 ships already completed: ...... ee. Nil

Ships still ON Order: occ eeee eens 3

Sy fat, only two companies, Maersk (see Regina
Maersk, Significant Ships of 1996) and P&O Nedlloyd, have chosen to push the capacity of their new con- tainer ships beyond the 6000TEU threshold. With the former company reluctant to disclose actual intakes, P&O Nedlloyd Southampton, with a quoted loading of
O690TEU has, at least on paper, a strong claim to be regarded as 'the world's largest’.

The design conforms to the now standard large container ship layout, with a short accommodation block and engineroom approximately three-quarters aft, and seven holds forward and one aft of this posi- tion. The forward holds are dimensioned to accept 2 x 40ft containers (4 x TEU) in cell guides, and each is closed by two sets of lift-on/off pontoon covers comprising three transverse panels, whilst the aft hold with three sets of covers, can accept 3 x 40ft contain- ets, but is otherwise restricted by the aft-body hull- form. Containers are carried on the hatch covers over the full width of the vessel, and an additional FEU tier can be carried aft of No 8 hold above the mootr- ing deck.

Lashing bridges are fitted on deck between the hatches, and these uniquely act as supports for 45ft con- tainers stowed across their tops and secured by twist- locks. Refrigerated cargoes are catered for by the provision of 710 electrical sockets. Maximum stowage on deck is 17 rows of 6TEU tiers, and in the holds, 15 tows and 9 tiers. The hatch coamings are continuous and form the major longitudinal strength members, and a double-hull structure surrounds the cargo space, forming part of a tank arrangement comprising side, bilge, and three transverse double-bottom compart- ments. One sidetank port and starboard is utilised for automatic heel adjustment, with water transferred by means of a ballast pump.

Where P&O Nedlloyd Southampton does successfully claim ‘largest’ is with choice of main engine: her Sulzer 1ZRTAY6C unit is believed to be the most powerful evet commissioned for marine use. Built in Japan by
Diesel United, this has an MCR rating of 89,640bhp (65,885kW) at 100rev/min and, when operating at 90% full power with a 20% sea margin and driving a shaft alternator as well, this produces a service speed of
 72
 24.5knots. A Geislinger torsional vibration damper is included in the propulsion line, together with a moni- toring system.

The shaft alternator is by Brush Electrical and devel- ops 3500kW, whilst four MaK/Brush diesel-powered sets each contribute 3600kW to an electrical operation controlled by a power management system, also designed by Brush.

Kelvin Hughes is the supplier of a NINAS 9000 inte- grated bridge system (IBS), including a console which includes three radars, multi-feature display (MFD), elec- tronic chart display and information service (ECDIS), and electronic plotting table (ECTAB). An engineroom monitoring station is also fitted on the bridge.

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF1998
PACIFICAT: speeding-up British Columbia's
 ferry links

Snipbullder: So eveeeeeaeeuaeaness Catamaran Ferries cae eveaeeseeseeaseeaeeseeeaess International, Canada
VESSEI'S NAME? oo... ccccece eee cence Pacificat
Hull number:
Owner/operator: British Columbia Ferry ee deecueeeeueeueeeueeuueeua eeu eeueeteneeta es Corp, Canada
DeSIQNE?: .........0.. Incat Designs, Australia/ eeu eeeeveseeeesueeeeuess Robert Allan Ltd, Canada ns eC re Canada
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ..............., Nil
Total number of sister

SNIOS STII] ON OEP: ... 0. cee ccecseeeee eee eee eee ees 2

COMBINATION of the regional government's
 perceived need to revitalise local marine industries by creating a base for the construction and marketing of fast ferries, and British Columbia Ferry Corp's require- ment to improve its almost saturated commuter services, led to an order for three fast catamaran-type ferries.
These were placed with the owner's shipbuilding sub- sidiary Catamaran Ferries International, as manager for the project. Construction of hull components was entrusted to some half-dozen participating yards within the province, with the various modules brought together on barges for assembly at CFI's North Vancouver ferry terminal.

Ferries and terminals around Vancouver and the
Georgia Strait are heavily congested by traffic move- ments, currently exceeding 22million passengers and eight million cars annually - mainly on the Vancouver-
Lower Mainland to Vancouver Island route. The plan is to use fast ferries to provide more frequent sailings for passengers and smaller vehicles - 12 trips daily as against eight by conventional ferries, by cutting journey time from 100 minutes to 65.

Based on technology developed by the Australian expert Incat, with local participation by Robert Allan, Pacificat is constructed of 5383-series marine grade alu- minium. The catamaran hullform provides a platform for two vehicle decks, the lower of which has bow and stern access ramps fabricated by Allied Shipbuilders, of
North Vancouver. The ferry’s stern has an unusual extension, as can be seen in the accompanying plan.
Together, these decks have a capacity for 250 cars and
 76
 up to 6 buses. The two passenger decks above are attractively laid out with compartmentalised lounges for 1000 passengers, an entertainments area, newstand, testaurant, cappuccino bar, snack bar, children's play area, gift shop, work cubicles with electrical outlets, and a lift serving all decks. A sheltered outdoor passenger deck is arranged aft.

Safety conforms to the IMO High-Speed Craft Code, and includes a new-generation marine escape system: the MEC II twin chute, developed by DBC Marine
Safety Systems. This equipment comprises two twin- chute installations each side of the vessel, which termi- nate at an inflatable buoyancy platform. The chutes are teleased by a pull handle, without any electrical power- ing, and evacuees make their exit down a zig-zag inner tube, so designed to give a controlled descent of around 4m/sec, before transferring from the platform at water level, into one of four 150-person reversible liferafts, available each side of the ship. Each chute can evacuate 400 persons in 17minutes, 40 seconds.

Pacificat is propelled at 37 knots by four Kamewa tran- som-mounted waterjets, powered through Renk gear- boxes by four 6500kW MTU 20V-1163 TB73L main engines running at 1250rev/min, positioned in pairs in the two waterborne hulls. Geislinger elastic damping couplings and composite shaftlines with Gesilco cou- plings are fitted in each transmission. Four Caterpil- lar/Leroy Somer 190kW diesel-alternator sets provide the electrical power.

HIS vessel is an example of China Shipbuilding's extensive series of medium-sized container ship.
Based on claims for the economic benefits of this design, represented by higher speed and loading perfor- mance, compared with similar designs, it has proved vety popular with operators worldwide. Rickmers, in particular, has taken delivery of a number of ships of this class, most of which, like Patricia Rickmers, have found ready employment on charter to liner companies.
Although the machinery room is placed right aft, the positioning of the deckhouse over the fore part of that space, and the squared-off aft deck, allows two blocks of 40ft containers to be carried in cell-guides aft of the accommodation. The main cargo space is forward of the machinery, and comprises five holds within a dou- ble-skin hull.

Fixed cell guides are fitted and total TEU intake is 2202 (including 300 refrigerated units), of which 870 are carried below deck. Nos 3,4,7 and 8 hatches are suit- able for 45ft containers, and dangerous goods may be loaded in the forward three holds. In addition, Nos 3 and 4 holds can be used for break-bulk cargoes such as coppet and zinc.

Side tanks formed within the double skin are used for water ballast and bunkers, with fuel also carried in cross- bunker tanks between Nos 2 and 3, and 4 and 5 holds.
Access tunnels are formed P&S at the top of the side tanks, a pair of which serve the anti-heeling system.
Three slim-line Tsuji electro-hydraulic deck cranes are mounted on the centreline between Nos 2 and 3, and 4 and 5 holds, and built into the back of the deckhouse block to serve the containers stowed on the aft deck.
Each is capable of lifting 45tonnes.

Hyundai has built the MAN B&W 7S70MC main engine, and this develops 20,870kW when running at 95rev/min. Service speed on the design draught is 20.50knots. Electrical supply comes from two 1200kW alternators, and two of 1000kW, all driven by MAN
B&W diesel engines. Kawasaki is the maker of a 1000kW bow thruster installed aft of the forepeak. A complement of 25 is accommodated in the seven-tier deckhouse, on top of which there is a wheelhouse laid
Out to suit one-man operation with a Sperry bridge con- trol system.

PEGASUS: a new container ship sub-series
 shipbuilder: ..Stocznia Gdynia SA, Poland

VESSEI'S NAMEL... ccc cceccceces seca eeee eens Pegasus
Hull NUMBES: occ ccceseces eee eeeeevenes 8138/5
Owner/operator:........... Alpha Ship GmbH, «100 vn ene vn 60s acm soem comme ave vail anane eons russ soee vatvL nw 8a Germany
Designer: ...... Stocznia Gdynia SA, Poland ll Germany
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ......... Nil
Total number of sister

SNIPS Stil] ON OFOer: 2... ee ceceeec eee eeeeee eens 5

ITH four deliveries completed from an order for
 10 vessels of Gdynia Shipyard's 8138 class of 1600TEU container ship, owner Alpha Ship began to find conditions in the charter market difficult for that size of vessel. As a result, the company exercised an option to lengthen the remaining six vessels by 16m, producing a more economical sub-series with the higher
TEU capacity of 1835 - an increase of 218TEU above that of the prototype 8138-class vessel Nadir (presented in Szgnificant Ships of 1997).

The four-hold layout of the earlier design has been retained, with the extra length added in the form of a new water ballast deeptank between Nos 2 and 3 holds (which also serves to limit the longitudinal bending moment in ballast conditions). Also, two more TEU stacks have been included in an extended No 2 hold - now served by three hatches. Further changes include moving the midships 45tonne crane to a position above the new deeptank, to provide a better balanced cargo space coverage, and re-arranging tanks to give increased ballast and fuel capacity; the latter enables an endurance of 17,000 nautical miles to be achieved.

The holds, within a double-hull structure, are dimen- sioned to suit 40ft containers carried within cell guides, and closed by two-panel pontoon covers with stack loadings of 80/110tonnes for TEU/FEU respectively.
On deck, lashing bridges between the hatches support the lower tier of containers, and a breakwater behind the forecastle protects those stowed forward. To meet the demands of charterers, the number of reefer con- tainers carried has been increased to 325, with 123 loaded in the holds where ventilation capacity has been enhanced.

The fine lines of the original 8138 design are undis- turbed, and allow the lengthened vessel to sail at a speed virtually unchanged from that of the prototype. Only at the bow do they restrict the positioning of the two lon- gitudinal girders within the double bottom, which form three transverse water ballast tanks. Despite this fine hull shape and the heavy deck structures, Pegasus exhibits good stability properties, with the deep free- board and close subdivision of the voids at the tops of the double-hull space satisfying the requirements for damage stability.

The main engine is a Cegielski-MAN B&W 6L70MC
Mk VI unit with an output of 17,200kW at 108rev/min, coupled directly to a FP propeller for a service speed of
 80
 21knots at 90% full power and with a sea margin of 15%. Electrical power is dertved from three Wartsila-
Dolmel diesel-alternator sets: one producing 930kW and two rated at 1240kW. ABB Zamech is the supplier of a 900kW bow thruster.

QUEEN OF THE

NETHERLANDS: world's largest trailing suction dredger
 shipbuilder: ............. Verolme Scheepswerf bveusnns sade ane vm Heusden BV, The Netherlands
Vessel's name:...Queen of the Netherlands
Hull NUMDES: oo. eee cce eee ese eve eeeenees 1030
Owner/operator:.............68 Royal Boskalis voeees Westminster NV, The Netherlands/UK
De@SIQNE: ........cceeee. Verolme Scheepswerf vocveeeeueueueuenes Heusden BV, The Netherlands
FIAQ! voce cecccceceeceee eee esneeeeenes The Netherlands
Total number of sister
 ships already completed’... 1
Total number of sister

SNIPS StI] ON OFC: wo... cece eeceee teen neees Nil

OUR years ago, Szgnificant Ships described Pearl

River as the ‘world's largest trailing suction dredger'. Now that claim is made for a vessel with both hopper capacity and deadweight some 25% eteater, reflecting the ever-increasing demand for dredging and transporting the maximum amount of material in the minimum amount of time. However, this title will be lost again quite soon when Thyssen

Nordseewerke delivers a 33,000m° capacity giant to ©

Jan de Nul early in 2000.

Queen of the Netherlands has a hullform developed by the Dutch tank Marin, which is a compromise between deadweight and speed requirements, and features a wide body with sleek lines. The 23,500m° hopper is contained within a double hull, and has side bulkheads which are sloped at top and bottom, and coamings forming torsion boxes. , 
A centreline box girder with topsides sloped at 45deg, separates two rows of 10 hydraulically operated bottom doors which provide one of three methods for dispos- ing of spoil. Alternatively, discharge can be made directly into a floating shore delivery line using a quick- connect ball-jointed unit on the forecastle head and a 10,000kW discharge pump, or accurate placement of material at great depths is possible via the suction pipes.

Operations are designed to comply with the recom- mendations of the 'Green Award Association’, particu- larly by adopting ‘closed-circuit dredging’ in which water used in the process is recirculated by the jet
 82
 pumps through the draghead, rather than being dumped over the side. Oveen of the Netherlands can dredge at depths up to 120m (aided by a submerged dredge pump fitted to the end of the suction pipe), and can operate efficiently either at her dredging draught of 11.46m, or in shallower water at a draught of 8.20m, using a pair of 1200mm suction pipes fitted with jet- water lines and trailed port and starboard from the for- watd end of the hopper.

Two 2800kW double-walled dredge pumps are installed in a forward pumptroom together with three 1000kW jet pumps - one low-pressure unit (which also supplies the bow jet) and two high-pressure ones.
Accurate positioning of the draghead during dredging is achieved by a 3D dynamic tracker which ts fully inte- erated with the bridge and navigation systems, as well as by a Simrad DP system; two Barkemeyer flap rud- ders; a 1000kW bow jet and 1400kW bow thruster.
Radio Holland supplied a multibeam echo sounder for underwater surveying and inspection.

Two Stork-Wartsila 12V46C main engines each pro- duce 12,600kW at 500rev/min, and drive CP propellers through flexible couplings and Renk gearboxes for a trial speed of 16.8knots. A flexible coupling at the fore side of each engine takes a drive to the two Holec 10,000kW main alternators, their output supplemented by a Wartsila/Holec 1800kW diesel unit.
