SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF1998
R-ONE: for a quieter form of cruising
 shipbuilder: ...Chantiers de I'Atlantique SA, eee er sey eres eye eeveeveeast ee France
VESSEI'S NAMEL...... 0. cecceeecee eee ees R-One (R71) mm 10]| 010100) 0\=) Gar H31
Owner/OPeratOr: .......cccccecceccseecseceeeeeueeeseeeens cveveeeevaeeneues Renaissance Cruises Inc, USA
Designer:.....Chantiers de I'Atlantique SA, Lede cece eee eee eeu sense seen ease eeeneeeeeneeeeeneeeneenes France
FAG! oo cecceecccecceeeceesseeeeseeueseeneeenstuestaness Liberia

SNips Still ON OFder: ...... cece cece eeeeeeee esses 5

RENMESAN CE Cruises has made it a speciality to cater for a clientele favouring the more relaxed atmosphere of small-ship luxury cruising, and although this new series is considerably larger than those of an existing fleet now in the process of disposal, the 702- passenger complement is still less than half that of most contemporary cruise liners. Such ships are still well suited for specialised cruising in such areas as the
South Seas, Indian Ocean and Mediterranean Sea, serv- ing mainly American tourists.

Passenger accommodation is distributed over eight decks, occupying an area of some 6000m? and includ- ing 348 cabins, 170 of which have private balconies. Six ‘owner's suites’ overlook the stern, with four more enjoying prominent positions across the front of decks 6 and 7. A number of cabins are fitted with pullman and sofa beds and only 25 have inside positions. Facilities provided for passengers have a fitness emphasis, with a large gymnasium, aerobics and treatment rooms avail- able on deck 9, alongside jacuzzis and a swimming pool.
A jogging track occupies much of deck 10, but quieter pursuits are catered for with a library and card rooms.

Passenger reception is on deck 4, with deck 5 the location of shops, a cabaret lounge and the high-class ‘formal’ restaurant with adjacent pre-dinner lounges.
The owner's policy of providing a first-class eating ser- vice is ensured by the availabity of three more restau- rants: the main, on deck 9 with buffet and barbecue, and two speciality dining rooms offering Italian and
American/Texan menus. Passenger access and stores movement on board is facilitated by an outfit of
MacGregor elevators, and the same company has designed and supplied the nine provision stores, as well
 86
 as the associated MacGregor-York centralised reftiger- ation plant.

A novel machinery layout has been adopted. The diesel-electric power installation utilises four Wartsila 12V32E diesel engines fitted line abreast in the main engineroom aft, but the drives from these pass forward through a bulkhead to an adjacent generator and propulsion room. Here they are coupled to the main alternators which supply both hotel services and the two Cegelec propulsion motors. A Geislinger elastic damping coupling and a flexible link coupling is fitted between each engine and its alternator.

The twin shaft lines from the 6750kW propulsion machines then pass back between each pair of main engines to drive FP propellers at 170rev/min for a set- vice speed of 20.2knots. Passenger comfort is enhanced with a pair of heeling tanks and retractable fin stabilisers. Two Brunvoll 750kW bow thrusters are fitted forward.

RAMFORM BANFF: the FPSO takes a new direction

Shipbuilder: ........ Hyundai Mipo Dockyard, eo deeeeeeueceueeaeeens Korea (Topsides fabrication ranma vara ede and outfit by Aker McNulty, UK)
VESSEI'S NAMEL...... eee Ramform Banff
FUL] NUIMIDES: oo. cec cece eee eee eee een een eeees 9701
Owner/operator:......... veeeeeeeees PGS Offshore

Designer (basis):...... Roar Ramde, Norway
FAQ: ..... bebe eee eeeeeeeeeeeeeeneeeeee ees Isle of Man
Total number of sister ships already completed:............ce ee, Nil
Total number of sister
SNIDOS Stil ON OFder: oo... cece cc eee cece eee eens Nil

HE Ramform concept was conceived by Norwe-
 cian naval architect Roar Ramde, and first applied in 1983 to a naval research vessel. Benefits of its unique delta-shaped hullform led PGS to order (from the Nor- wegian Langsten group) a number of seismic survey vessels to the design, and to later acquire exclusive licensing rights. Advantages of cost and building time were amongst the reasons for the adoption of this B- 380 derivative for use as an FPSO serving Conoco's
Banff (Central North Sea) oil field.

The exceptionally high beam/length ratio resulting from the delta shape provides a stable work platform of large area, on to which a 3500tonne process facility with a daily capacity of 60,000barrels of oil and 90,000bar- tels of fluid has been installed. Also fitted on deck are gas dehydration (compression and export), water injec- tion, process, well control and shutdown systems.
Accommodation for a complement of 40 persons is provided forward of frame 90 (near midships), with a helideck arranged at the bow. As well as its delta shape resulting in a block coefficient of only 0.471, Ramform
 88

Banff exhibits a most unusual midship section, as shown in the accompanying plan.

For safety reasons, production facilities are separated from the accommodation area by a blast wall at the aft side of the deckhouse (frame 90), with a blast tunnel on the port side forming an escape route between the two areas. Additionally, a vertical segregation of systems means that only one side of the vessel carries hydrocar- bons. This blast wall also divides the hull underdeck, with the fore part given over to machinery and thruster compartments, and the aft body forming five storage and two slop tanks, with a total capacity of approxi- mately 19,000m?* (120,000barrels).

A fully weather-vaning turret system with a Brown
Brothers swivel stack is positioned amidships, and this provides a 10-line mooring configuration to keep the vessel on station in a 90m water depth. It also has a capacity for 15 risers (5 x 12in, 10 x 81in, or 5 x 305mm, 10 x 203mm). In the absence of a dynamic positioning system, automatic heading control is provided from three azimuthing thrusters.

An interesting adjunct to position-keeping is the aetodynamically-shaped wind-sail, which also carries the flare tower. A hydraulically-controlled flap at the back of the sail is controlled and monitored from the bridge, and can be turned through +35deg to vary side- ways thrust from wind forces, tn order to adjust the heading of the ship and reduce the use of thruster power.

Three 2800kW Lips fixed-pitch/variable speed and azimuthine thrusters, port, starboard and centre at the stern, form part of the Cegelec integrated power and propulsion system. This is centred on twin Wartsila-
Sulzer 16V25 main engines (from the Nohab factory), each developing 3860kW at 900rev/min and directly coupled to two 3540kW Leroy-Somer alternators. A large amount of additional power, mainly for the pro- cessing plant, is derived from two ABB Stal GT35 gas turbine-driven packages, each having an output of
 17,000kW.

Te latest addition to South Coast's fleet of dredgers is engaged in aggregate recovery from
Western European waters for use by the UK and
Continental construction industries, and has a capacity almost twice that of the company's earlier vessels but on an almost idential length bp, indicative of the increasing demand for this material. The profile is sin- gle-decked with poop, forecastle, and a large hopper hold shaped to suit the vertically mounted bucket dis- charge wheel, and contained within a double-hull space containing fuel and fresh water tanks, voids and fore- and-aft protected passageways P&S.

Unlike two smaller vessels built by Merwede for this owner (for example, Sand Heron, featured in
Significant Ships of 1990), Sand Falcon has a twin-sctew machinery installation based on a pair of Wartsila
Vasa 6R32E main engines, each having an MCR of 2460kW at 750rev/min, driving twin CP propellers at 199rev/min through Lohmann & Stolterfoht gear- boxes with highly elastic, pneumatically controlled, double-cone friction clutches. Electrical supply comes from a 2860kVA Leroy Somer alternator dri- ven from the front end of each main engine, and from two Cummins/Leroy Somer 312.5kVA diesel- driven sets. |

Dredging is carried out by means of a single trailing suction pipe which can be suspended over the port side to depths up to 33m, by three hinged gantries. Its flexibity allows the California-type draghead to remain flat on the sea bed in all conditions, and a swell compensator provides 4m of overall movement at the draghead suspension point. This equipment, and the 2350kW dredge pump, was supplied by Seadrec, and delivers the dredged material to slewing, screen-type loading towers, located on the starboard side, for dis- tribution over the hopper. Each tower has a sand chute at one end and a hinged stone or gravel chute at the other.
 90
 dredger

The double-walled dredge pump is installed in the forward pumproom and is driven, through a gearbox and pneumatically operated clutch, by an electric motor mounted in an adjacent compattment which also con- tains the drive for a 6tonne bow thruster. A high-lift tudder is fitted behind each propeller and assists manoeuvrability by directing propeller thrust.

Dry discharge from the hopper at 1300m°/h is by means of a Strachan & Henshaw bucket-wheel unloader fitted with 10 x 1000litre buckets, which stows forward and is hydraulically driven along tracks on the hopper coaming. Conveyor belts transport the material to a hinged discharge boom at the starboard poop front, thence to shore or into barges.
Alternatively, the dredge pump can be used to trans- fer direct from the hopper to shore via pipeline, whilst straight dumping can be carried out through eight hydraulically operated bottom doors. Complete control of the operation is maintained from the dredging position at the port side of the wheelhouse, which gives the dredging master an overview of the
 hopper.

SCHIEHALLION: showcase for UK offshore fabrication

Shipbuilder:.Harland & Wolff Shipbuilders
 deste & Heavy Industries Ltd (HWSHI), UK
VESSEl'S NAME? ......cceeeceee eee eees Schiehallion
Hull NUMDES: o.oo. cece ce eee cece eee eeeeeeneennes 1737
Owner/operatOr:...........c00ce BP Exploration ae ees eee Operating Co Ltd, UK
DESIQNETS!.......:ccceeeeeeeeeeeeees Harland & Wolff locccueeeeeeueeeaeeeseeeeeaeeeneeeseeneeens and Associates ic 0 ee Not flagged
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: ...........cceeeeees Nil
Total number of sister

Ships still ON Order: oo... cee eeeeeeeeee eee Nil

NLY five yeats passed between the discovery of
 the North-West Atlantic Schiehallion oilfield in 1993 and the start of oil recovery, with construction of this vessel taking little more than 20 months from the time of project sanction. As designer and builder of the hull, HWSHI has worked with international leaders Brown & Root (topsides facilities), Coflexip
Stena (flexible risers) and Single Buoy Moorings in developing what is understood to be the world's largest
FPSO.

In service, Schzehallion will operate above the field's four main drilling centres, secured by 14 anchor legs made up of 160mm diameter studless chain and wire rope, fixed to anchor piles arranged within a 1650m radius. Rigid steel flowlines and flexible risers connect production and water injection wells at each centre to the FPSO, with the risers laid in a ‘pliant wave' format to cope with the harsh environmental conditions. The moorings and tisets ate attached to a 14m diameter top-mounted internal turret inserted through the hull at the fore end. This allows ‘weathervane' movement around the anchored position and can accommodate up to 24 risers, as well as tensioning mooring winches, manifold and swivel arrangement.

From the turret, recovered fluids pass to production facilities with a peak capacity of 154,000bbl/day, mounted on skids 4m above deck level, forward of the accommodation block. ‘These comprise two cas/oil/water separation trains which clean and re- inject gas and water, and also chemically treat the undet-sea reservoirs and facilities using processes which mimimise environmental impact and maximise energy recovery.

The hull is a simple barge-type structure with a high forecastle to protect the processing areas and mooring turret. A double skin at the sides forms water ballast

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 tanks, coated with Jotun WG Balloxy HB light epoxy, used for maintaining stability, whilst a centreline bulk- head allows seven pairs of cargo tanks and two slop tanks (total 950,000bbl) to be arranged within the hull.
These provide storage for crude oil from the produc- tion trains, prior to off-loading in 600,000bbl/24h packages (5600m°/h) to shuttle tankers (see Loch
Rannoch page 66) moored 80m astern, using an outfit of
Framo deepwell pumps.

Two Schottel 1500kW azimuthing thrusters are fitted under the stern, and work in conjunction with a Simrad dynamic heading and thruster control system which facilitates operational heading whilst offloading. They can also provide limited self-propulsion duties.
Electrical power is derived from two gas turbine-driven 43MW GEC-Alstom (formerly known as Alsthom) alternators, installed on the process deck and fitted with dual-fuel prime movers supplied by European Gas
Turbines.

SEA-LAND ARGENTINA: new box-ship series
 shipbuilder:....... Daewoo Heavy Industries soca ee eeeeeeeeeeeeeuveeaeeveneuseueresneatuenernens Ltd, Korea
Vessel's name: ........... Sea-Land Argentina
Hull NUMDE|S: oo. cece ece cece eee eee eevee seen es 4053
Owner/operator: Conti Castillo Schiffahrts
Loveeeeees GmbH & Co KG (NSB Niederelbe), cece ee eeeeeee eee eeu ees eeeeueeeee eee eetaeeenneeans Germany

SHIOS Still ON OFC: 0. eee cece eee eeee eee eenes 4
 his new group of six vessels, chartered to Sea-

Land, adopts an all-aft configuration (apparently the first time a Far Eastern builder has done this for a ship of this size and type), thereby maximising stowage space and box-handling capabilities. The ftve holds are served by 10 hatches, with Nol hatch positioned on the long forecastle. All are fitted with pontoon covers and
Nos1 and 2 are arranged for the carriage of dangerous soods of IMDG classes 1.45, 2, 3, 4, 5.1, 6.1, 8 and 9.
A total of 200 refrigerated TEU can be stowed two tiers high on covers 7 to 10, and one tier high on cov- ers 4,5 and 6.

The design features a seven-tier deckhouse built on a short poop right aft: in consequence, deck space 1s available forward of this for 3 x TEU bays above the engineroom, with stowage arranged within cell guides up to the third of seven tiers. A maximum of six tiers/12 rows of containers can be carried on the hatch covers, and 6/10 in the holds. The vessels are self-sus- taining using three MacGregor-Hagelund 40tonne cranes mounted on the centreline.

The cargo space is enclosed within a double-skin hull utilised for water ballast tanks, except at midships where No3 P&S tanks carry fuel, and No4 P&S tanks serve additionally as heeling tanks, using a 450m°/h pump to restrict heel to within 2dee whilst working containers. A midships deeptank, four frame spaces long, also carries fuel, and the tops of the double-hull contain fore and aft passageways. The full complement is 26 plus six Suez crew, and it is noted that measures adopted in the design to alleviate noise and vibration problems, resulting from the positioning of the deck- house, have proved successful. :

The main engine is a Hanjung-Sulzer ORTA72U unit developing 24,420bhp at 97rev/min MCR, directly

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 coupled to a four-bladed FP propeller. Running at 90%
MCR output, designed service speed is 21knots, how- ever, trial results proved an extra 0.5knots was attain- able at this power. Electrical requirements are served from three diesel-driven 1170kW alternator sets, and there is a 1100kW bow thruster fitted with a CP pro- peller.

SEAQUEEN: Chinese-built products tanker

Shipbuilder:...Dalian New Shipyard, China

VESSEl'S NAMEL.... cece eecee cece ee eens Seaqueen

Garey efficiency in operation, and easy mainte- nance are the noteworthy features claimed for this series of quality products tankers, two of which have been classed by Lloyd's Register and one by American
Bureau of Shipping, giving rise to the claim that they are the first vessels to simultaneously satisfy the requirements of both societies - and especially their
ShipRight and SafeHull notations. By fitting corru- gated longitudinal and centreline bulkheads, external deck stiffening, and tank side structure within the dou- ble-hull space, the cargo tanks are smooth for easy cleaning, whilst for corrosion protection they have been given three coats of pure epoxy paint following stripe coats and two-pass grinding. Similar treatments, but using modified epoxy, have been applied to decks and ballast tanks.

Additionally, only 30% of the steel content is of the high-tensile type with its use limited to grade AH32.
Plate thicknesses have been increased above rule at oil/ballast boundaries, and Swedish 'Z' quality plating with enhanced through-thickness properties adopted for lower bulkhead stool tops. The use of Swedish- made bulb plates in ballast tanks has improved paint application and future maintenance.

There are six pairs of cargo tanks within a double- skin hull which forms side tanks with top and bottom wing hoppers. These are common with a centrally divided double bottom and utilised for water ballast.
Double valve segregation throughout allows two and three cargo parcels to be carried. Other features of the piping system include sea and fresh water connec- tions to drop lines in No1 tank for recirculation, flush- ing and rinsing of all cargo piping; stainless steel heating coils; segregated stripping system to prevent mixing of vapours from different cargoes; a dedicated ballast stripping system, and GRP ballast piping. Bal- last tanks and voids can be inerted and purged, and, with the pumproom, are fitted for permanent gas sampling.

The design parameters required a maximum cubic to suit a deadweight of around 110,000tonnes on 15.50m draught, and an optimised service speed with the main engine running at 85% MCR on a draught of 14.9m, and corresponding deadweight of 105,000tonnes. The speed thus attained is 15knots, derived from a Dalian-
Sulzer 7RTA62U main engine developing 21,140bhp
MCR at 113rev/min, and driving a five-bladed FP pro- peller fitted behind a wake-equalisation duct. The three
Yanmar/Taiyo 870kW diesel-alternator sets are each capable of sustaining the sea load, and two 35tonne/h boilers deal with steam requirements. Two inert-gas gen-
 98
 erators are fitted and give 100% redundancy. Single- point mooring fittings are provided, with manoeuvrabil- ity when connecting to an offshore hose facilitated bya 1000kW bow thruster.

SEGERO: advanced Korean cable ship
 shipbuilder: ......... Hanjin Heavy Industries sc ccuecueuceeeeeaeeneuteteneeeuueesueeeteens Co Ltd, Korea
VeSSEl'S NAME? oo... ec ceeceece cece eeeeeeeeeees Segero
HUll NUMBEDL ooo. cece cece cece eee eeeeee eee eens N-058
Owner/operator.............. Korea Submarine cccueeueueeeeesueeneueueeeens Telecom Co Ltd, Korea
DESIGNED ......cceccececce cece eeeee eet entree neste neeeeaneetin - ic (0 eee ee Panama
Total number of sister
 ships already completed: .......... ee. Nil
Total number of sister

Ships Still ON OFer ........ ee eeeeeeeee steer eee es Nil
 'EGERO has been designed to lay, bury and repair
 intercontinental submarine fibre-optical cable, with particular reference to the severe weather conditions experienced in the North Pacific. She continues the fully stern-wotking concept adopted for cable layers in recent years - an arrangement which allows work to be catried out in the worst conditions, and permits a con- ventional bow-form to be adopted, giving higher cruis- ing speeds.

All cable work is concentrated on a continuous main deck almost totally enclosed by a long forecastle which forms the upper deck, using port and starboard cable lines, each having a 4m diam/ 40tonne-pull drum engine fitted with a three-motor DC electric drive combination, and a four-wheel-pair draw-off/hold- back linear cable engine. This equipment, together with a two-wheel-pair cable transporter, has been sup- plied by Dowty-Precision Handling Systems, from the
UK.

The package is designed to lay cable at speeds up to 6knots over two 3.6m diameter stern sheaves. These are straddled by a 35tonne SWL A-frame capable of work- ing in an arc ranging from 80deg over the stern to 80deg inboard, which provides for launching and recovering a cable plough from either cable line. The working area is also served by a 20tonne telescopic, and an 8tonne artic- ulated deck crane.

Elsewhere on this deck there are test rooms and workshops, and two repeater stacks spray-cooled by chilled fresh water. These are fitted over the two main cable tanks of total capacity 2050m’ (3900tonnes). ‘Two 80m? cable tanks are arranged between these main tanks. Accommodation for 63 personnel is concen- trated in a deckhouse block built about midships on the upper deck, with space forward and aft of this available for buoy stowage, and modular units used for cable plough control and ROV operations.
 100

The extensive navigating bridge is given over to a combined wheelhouse/control centre with full-width view forward and aft. A Kongsberg Simrad integrated monitoring and control system is fitted, incorporating dynamic positioning; thruster and vessel control; func- tions related to cable laying and trenching; joystick oper- ation of thrusters; and power management. A STN
Atlas total navigation system is also provided.

The diesel-electric power system is based on four
ABB alternators: 2 x 3400kW; 1 x 2300kW; and 1 x 850kW, driven by MAN B&W engines. Power is dis- tributed through two switchboards and four trans- formers to 2 x 2700kW ABB motors driving Kamewa azimuthing propulsion thrusters aft. To provide pro- tection against cable fouling and also to develop a min- imum bollard pull of 80tonnes, these are fitted in steerable nozzles. Two Lips 1200kW tunnel thrusters and a White-Gill vectoring unit operate forward, and all propulsion and thruster motors are speed-con- trolled by AC frequency converters which provide immediate response and control for manoeuvring per- formance.

LTHOUGH ordered by SweFerry, a subsidiary of

Swedish State Railways, Sane was delivered into the Scandlines consortium when completed in Decem- ber 1997 by AESA's Puerto Real shipyard, now a sepa- rate entity within the Group, following the recent pattial-privatisation and re-organisation. Claimed to be the largest passenger, car, trailer and train ferry in the wotld, this vessel operates a schedule encompassing five round trips each day with only a 75 minute turn-round time, between Rostock and Trelleborg.

For this service, manoeuvring within the restricted port access, which includes a long stern-first approach at Trelleborg, is enhanced by the fitting of three
Kamewa 1500kW bow thrusters, and a bow rudder formed of hydraulically-operated flaps. These flank the bulbous bow and stow flush to the shell when not in use. Power for the thrusters comes from two 3000kW alternators, driven from two twin-input/single-output geatboxes, through which four MAN B&W 8L48/60 main engines supply the twin CP propellers. Three 2150kW diesel-alternator sets satisfy the service and hotel loading. A comprehensive automation system operated from the bridge, controls and monitors the main machinery, fire fighting and damage control, and utilises more than 7000 channels.

Rail access is over the stern only at deck 3, where there are six tracks separated into three sections of two tracks by two half-height longitudinal bulkheads, as part of recent efforts to improve ro-ro ship damage stability. These are fitted with hydraulically operated flood control doors forward and aft, which allow traf- fic access into the side sections, and enable road vehi- cles to turn at the forward end through 180deg¢ for exit. Because Sane is wider than other ferries on the route, the rail tracks are aligned to an opening at the statboatd side of the stern. This is closed by a verti- cally sliding door, and is only 10m wide to suit the existing linkspan, where hydraulic cylinders ensure that tracks are aligned and the vessel held tight when berthed.

A huge 104m long two-tier train lift, designed by
MacGregor and operated by hydraulic cylinders, extends over the midship body on deck 3, encom- passing the centre pair of tracks. This can accommo- date two complete trains with a total weight of 816tonnes, and lower them in about three minutes to tank top level to provide additional stowage. Although toad vehicles can be carried on deck 3, decks 5 and 7 above ate specifically for this type of transport, loaded through horizontally-sliding side doors, for-

SIGNIFICANT SHIPS OF 1998
 ward and aft, starboard on deck 5. A hoistable/tilting ramp on the port side, hinged at either end, links the two decks.

Decks 8 and 9 accommodate 600 passengers in 150 cabins, each fitted with two single beds and two pullman berths. Public facilities include a health club; sauna; cin- ema; lounge; cafeteria; dining room for lorry drivers; children's playroom; bar; and shops.

OMPETITION between the two principal players
 on the popular Patras/Ancona crossing, between
Greece and Italy, saw each of them introduce new classes of fast passenger/vehicle ferries to the route in 1995 (Superfast 1 and Aretousa, featured in Significant Ships of 1995). Now Superfast, whose earlier newbuildings were already faster than the opposition, has added almost two knots to the service speed for a new pair of vessels, allowing a 48h round-trip to be comfortably achieved, even in rough weather.

The hullform has been especially developed for good seaworthiness and manoeuvrability, and to suit a service speed of 28.5knots (full speed is more than 30 knots) produced from four 10,560kW/ 510rev/min Sulzer 16Z.AV40S main engines. Two Schelde gearboxes trans- mit power to twin CP propellers, and also drive a pair of 1780kVA ABB alternators. Three Wartsila/ABB 1460kW diesel- alternator sets are additionally fitted, and to further improve manoeuvring, a 1000kW tunnel- type stern thruster has been added to the two bow units fitted in the earlier vessels.

Despite an increase in dimensions, passenger capacity is the same as in the earlier Superfast ferries: total capac- ity 1400, accommodated in 222 cabins supplied in mod- ular form from the shipbuilder's Pukkio factory. These ptovide 750 beds, with an additional 60 couchettes also available. Public rooms, mainly on deck 7, include 4a la carte and self-service restaurants, disco lounge, casino, bar lounge, children's playroom, boutique, duty-free shop and reception lounge.

Commercial vehicle drivers have their own lounge, and outside facilities include an open deck bar and a

Bee dky
 ik ost ery ye east
 swimming pool with bar. MacGregor has supplied the passenger access doots at the stern and at the ship's side on deck 7, together with escalators and elevators in the passenger and crew accommodation.

The hydraulically operated cargo access/transfer equipment has also been supplied by MacGregor, to suit a through-loading application with four fixed and two hoistable car decks, centrally divided by partial engine and access casings. Forward, a lateral-opening, twin-leaf weathertight bow doot, gives entry to No 3 deck over a three-section ramp, whose inner section forms a watetr- tight door at the collision bulkhead when raised, with the outer section folding behind the bow door.

A hinged watertight door behind this arrangement forms an extra closure to the main garage space. At the stern there are two watertight ramp/dootrs for vehicles, and one for passengers. The vehicle doors have 8m (starboard) and 5m (port) wide driveways leading through 4.70m high openings into the main garage space, from which a series of hinged/tilting and fixed ramps link with the other vehicle decks to accommo- date 160 cars and 122 trailers.
